LeadPage's $effect (22-24) reloads per routed key; App.svelte:376 mounts LeadPage without {#key}, and the store is a module singleton, so in-flight promises write stale results even across unmount/remount. The draft-seeding effect is at lines 67-80 (finding said 68-80, off by one); combined with saveNote/saveContact targeting the prop displayClusterId (145-151), a stale lead-A arrival under route B re-seeds drafts from A and one Save click writes A's notes/contact onto B — confirmed corruption path with no error and no undo. No mitigations: no AbortController/sequencing anywhere in app/src/lib, no leads-store unit test (only leads-api.test.ts), e2e suite stale (#92). Not a duplicate of the 2026-06-07 perf audit (LeadPage appears there only in PERF-012 lazy-import and the searchOwners scan item). Nuance: when stale A lands the whole page renders A (header included), so the user believes they are on lead A — the write still lands on B (the URL key), making it silent corruption; exploitation needs out-of-order response completion (plausible: lead-detail payload size varies from one parcel to hundreds of holdings, prod is remote Fly) plus a navigation or save inside the window, so the wrong-lead render is the common symptom and the cross-lead save the rarer-but-severe one. Proposed fix (request-key/id guard, or assign only when result.owner.display_cluster_id matches the routed key) is sound and should also cover pursue(); for merge/split the response id matches the survivor key so id-matching works there too. High severity is fair given notes/contact are the product's core work artifact and the overwrite is permanent and undetectable.

Streaming infra→parcel pre-pass hard-codes predicate="within", silently dropping polygon solar→parcel matches the registered join deliberately catches with "intersects"

scripts/preprocess/compose.py:172-179 · reviewer: preprocess-core
What it is
compose.py's chunked path replaces the three registered InfraToParcelJoin instances with one shared shard sweep (_build_infra_to_parcel_lookups), and that sweep uses gpd.sjoin(..., predicate="within") for every layer. But the registered SolarToParcelJoin it supersedes (compose.py lists "SolarToParcelJoin" in joins_applied so the registered version never runs in chunked mode) was deliberately declared with predicate="intersects" because solar facilities are polygons: joins/infra_to_parcel.py says "Solar facilities may be polygons (utility-scale) — use intersects so a field on multiple parcels reports its primary parcel hit", and the USPVDB normalizer confirms the canonical solar_facility layer is polygon footprints ("USGS USPVDB (shapefile) → canonical `solar_facility` (polygons)"). A polygon solar field that crosses any parcel boundary is not strictly `within` a single parcel, so it gets zero matches in the streaming sweep.
Why it matters
Utility-scale solar footprints routinely straddle parcel lines, so this silently drops a large share of solar→parcel attachments: the solar row loses parcel_owner/parcel_id_full, the parcel never gets has_solar/solar_count/an infrastructure_summary entry, and — because the owners-index Lead universe is `has_tower | has_solar` (_read_state_parcels in owners_index.py) — entire solar-hosting landowners vanish from the Leads product. Slice C's solar→parcel work is undermined without any error or warning.
How to fix
Make the predicate per-layer in _build_infra_to_parcel_lookups: extend the specs dict to carry (infra, keep_cols, predicate) and pass "within" for tower/billboard, "intersects" for solar (mirroring the registered join declarations, or better, read the predicate off the registered InfraToParcelJoin instances so the two paths can't drift again). Recompose affected states afterward.
Expected improvement
Polygon solar facilities regain their parcel match; has_solar/solar_count become accurate; solar-hosting owners reappear in owners_index.json and the Candidates list.
Evidence
compose.py: `hit = gpd.sjoin(infra_geo, right, how="inner", predicate="within")` (one predicate for all layers) vs joins/infra_to_parcel.py: `# Solar facilities may be polygons (utility-scale) — use intersects ...` / `InfraToParcelJoin(target_type="solar_facility", ..., predicate="intersects")`; uspvdb.py docstring: "→ canonical `solar_facility` (polygons)"
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): VERIFIED in current code. Read scripts/preprocess/compose.py (full), scripts/preprocess/joins/infra_to_parcel.py (full), scripts/preprocess/normalizers/uspvdb.py, scripts/preprocess/geom.py:54-90, scripts/preprocess/owners_index.py:505-528, tests/test_infra_to_parcel.py (grep), docs/PERF_AUDIT_2026-06-07.md PERF-016 section, and git history (f1141e4, f1f9ff3, c47e184). Every mechanical claim holds: (1) compose.py:176 hard-codes `predicate="within"` for all three infra layers in the shared shard sweep (finding's line range 172-179 is correct); (2) compose.py:383 lists "SolarToParcelJoin" in joins_applied and line 389 skips any join in that list, so the registered intersects-join never executes — and since the single-shot compose path was retired (f1f9ff3), the within-sweep is the ONLY solar→parcel path in production; (3) joins/infra_to_parcel.py:81-93 registers SolarToParcelJoin with predicate="intersects" plus the exact comment quoted; (4) uspvdb.py keeps raw polygon geometries (add_polygon_derivatives in geom.py preserves main geometry, centroid is a separate column), so the sweep's left frame is polygons and polygon-within-polygon misses any solar field crossing a parcel line; (5) owners_index.py:_read_state_parcels (lines 508-528) confirms the Lead universe is has_tower|has_solar, so missed solar attachments remove leads (function name in finding is correct). No mitigation found anywhere: no centroid conversion before the sweep, no warning, and the only test of the intersects intent (tests/test_infra_to_parcel.py:107 test_solar_join_uses_intersects_predicate, which constructs exactly the boundary-straddling polygon "within would miss; intersects hits") covers the registered Join class that no longer runs — so the test passes while production behavior diverges. That test is strong evidence intersects-for-solar is deliberate, tested intent, making this a genuine invariant violation, not a design choice. NOT a duplicate of the 42 perf-audit findings: PERF-016 is the consolidation refactor and its work order said "Output identical" — accurately, because the pre-PERF-016 chunked code (c47e184^ compose.py:157) already hard-coded within. This is a correctness bug, absent from the audit. One framing correction: the finding implies the shared-sweep refactor introduced the supersession. Actually the chunked pre-pass has hard-coded "within" since chunked compose landed (f1141e4, 2026-05-26, chunked_compose.py:270), when only FL/NC used it; the real regression for AR/MT came when the single-shot path (which DID run the registered intersects join) was retired in f1f9ff3. This slightly changes the blame trail but not the current-code description, impact, or fix. The proposed fix (per-layer predicate in the specs dict, ideally read off the registered join instances) is correct and low-risk; severity high is justified — silent lead-universe shrinkage in a shipped Slice-C feature (CLAUDE.md: "solar→parcel done") with zero warnings, though actual magnitude depends on what fraction of anchor-state USPVDB footprints straddle parcel lines (unverifiable without the git-ignored data, but utility-scale fields straddling lines is geometrically routine and the code's own comment + dedicated test anticipate it).

fcc_uls_market nested band layout is invisible to the default load_state_data — PCS/AWS/700MHz locations never reach compose

scripts/preprocess/normalizers/fcc_uls.py:640, 703-718 · reviewer: preprocess-normalizers
What it is
FccUlsMarketNormalizer writes its canonical output one band per sub-directory (`band_dir = (out_dir / band) if nest else out_dir`), producing normalized/fcc_uls_market/{pcs,aws,700mhz}/location.parquet (and entity/license). But neither normalizer overrides Normalizer.load_state_data, and the default loader (base.py:211-218) only looks in `normalized/<source_id>/<basename>/*.parquet` or `normalized/<source_id>/<basename>.parquet` — i.e. a `location/` subdir or a root `location.parquet`, neither of which exists for the market source. So compose._load_state_data silently loads zero market-band rows; only fcc_uls_cellular's root-level location.parquet is found. The UlsToAsrJoin docstring explicitly relies on the composer loading locations ("``location`` carries ``state_fips`` (per-state) so it rides in the per-state ``ComposeContext``") and only special-cases the *license* table for direct disk reads (_LICENSE_RELPATHS), confirming the author believed market locations flow through the loader.
Why it matters
Three of the four Phase-1 carrier bands (PCS, AWS, 700 MHz — the bands T-Mobile, Dish and much of AT&T/Verizon spectrum live in) contribute zero locations to the ULS↔ASR carrier join. Per the #11 audit cited in uls_to_asr.py, those bands have 0% asr_number coverage and were expected to match via the spatial fallback — instead they are silently absent. Carrier lists on towers systematically under-report, and the Slice-A coverage metric measures only the cellular band. No error or warning is ever emitted (an empty per_source dict is simply skipped in compose.py:632).
How to fix
Override load_state_data on FccUlsMarketNormalizer to iterate the band sub-directories (glob `normalized/fcc_uls_market/*/{location,entity}.parquet` and concat, filtering by state_fips), or change _normalize(nest=True) to write the sharded layout the default loader already understands (e.g. `location/pcs.parquet`, `location/aws.parquet`, ...) and update _LICENSE_RELPATHS in joins/uls_to_asr.py accordingly. Add a tests/test_compose_loader.py case with a nested-band fixture so the layout contract is pinned.
Expected improvement
PCS/AWS/700MHz ULS locations actually participate in the carrier join, raising carrier coverage on towers to what #11 calibrated for; the loader layout contract becomes test-enforced.
Evidence
fcc_uls.py:640 `band_dir = (out_dir / band) if nest else out_dir`; base.py:213-217 `subdir = src_dir / basename ... single = src_dir / f"{basename}.parquet"`; uls_to_asr.py:57 "``location`` carries ``state_fips`` (per-state) so it rides in the per-state ``ComposeContext``"

eia_860 writes every EIA plant (coal/gas/nuclear included) as a canonical solar_facility — left-merge with no technology filter

scripts/preprocess/normalizers/eia_860.py:53-75 · reviewer: preprocess-normalizers
What it is
The module docstring promises "Other generation technologies are excluded — this is the solar pipeline", but the code never filters. It left-merges the Solar sheet onto the full Plant sheet (`merged = plant.merge(solar[...], how="left")`) and then emits a canonical solar_facility row for every row of `merged` (`for _, r in merged.iterrows()`). Non-solar plants get tech="" and, because the tracking heuristic defaults, `tracking_type="fixed"`. If no Solar xlsx is found at all (`solar_xlsx is None`), the entire Plant sheet still becomes solar facilities. Contrast eia_860m.py:49-50, which correctly filters `df["Technology"].str.contains("Solar")`.
Why it matters
Every US utility-scale power plant in the EIA-860 Plant file (~12k, all fuels) lands in the canonical solar_facilities table. Downstream, SolarToParcelJoin and the per-parcel infra summary set has_solar=True / solar_count>0 on parcels hosting any power plant, the interestingness score and Lead surfaces show phantom solar, and CountyScoreToSolarJoin / county summaries count non-solar plants as solar facilities.
How to fix
Filter `merged` to plants that actually appear in the Solar sheet (inner-merge on Plant Code, or keep only rows where the joined Technology is non-null / contains "Solar"), mirroring the eia_860m approach. If the Solar xlsx is missing, fail loudly rather than emitting the whole Plant sheet. Add a fixture-backed test (there is currently no tests/test_eia_860.py).
Expected improvement
solar_facilities contains only solar plants; has_solar/solar_count on parcels and all solar-derived scoring become trustworthy.
Evidence
eia_860.py docstring: "Other generation technologies are excluded"; line 53 `merged = plant.copy()`; lines 54-55 `merged = plant.merge(solar[...], how="left", ...)`; line 75 `for _, r in merged.iterrows():` with no Technology filter before `rows.append`

fetch_one accepts cancel_check but never uses it — mid-fetch cancellation is inert, and download() would retry a cancel anyway

scripts/fetch/puller.py:97-144 · reviewer: fetch-review
What it is
fetch_one declares `cancel_check: Callable[[], bool] | None = None` in its signature (puller.py:101) and the desktop puller passes the live cancel flag into it (app_puller/workers.py:104 `cancel_check=lambda: self._cancel`), but the body of fetch_one never references the parameter — it calls `source.fetch(partial, progress)` with no cancellation path, and `DataSource.fetch` has no cancel parameter either. The only place cancellation works is between sources in FetchWorker.run. Separately, http.download() DOES support cancel_check (http.py:171 raises `HttpError("download cancelled by user")`), but no source passes it, and even if wired, the cancel raise is an HttpError, which download()'s retry loop catches as a transient network error (http.py:136) and retries the whole download up to 3 more times (1+4+16s backoff) before surfacing.
Why it matters
Clicking Cancel during a long pull does nothing until the entire source finishes. For NC OneMap that is a 30-60 minute, 1,186-request fetch; for fcc_uls a_lease_608 a ~275 MB download. The worker QThread keeps the network loop running (stop_thread only quit+waits 2s), so the user either waits it out or kills the app — and killing the app mid-promote is exactly the failure window the atomic-write design tries to avoid. The latent retry-on-cancel in http.download means even a future wiring of cancel_check would re-open the connection 3 more times after the user cancels.
How to fix
Minimal fix with no DataSource signature change: in fetch_one, wrap the progress callback — `def _progress(done, tot, name): if cancel_check and cancel_check(): raise FetchCancelled(); progress(done, tot, name)` — since the contract already requires every fetch() to call progress while streaming. Define FetchCancelled as a plain Exception subclass (NOT HttpError) so http.download's retry `except (TimeoutError, HttpError, urllib.error.URLError, OSError)` does not swallow it; also change http.py:172 to raise that same non-retryable type instead of HttpError. Mark the resulting FetchOutcome as cancelled rather than fetch_failed in the manifest.
Expected improvement
Cancel actually stops a fetch within one progress tick (≤64 KiB chunk or one NC page), the .partial dir is left for the resume path where supported, and a cancelled download no longer burns 21 seconds of retries.
Evidence
puller.py:101 `cancel_check: Callable[[], bool] | None = None,` … body: `result = source.fetch(partial, progress)` (no other reference to cancel_check); http.py:171-172 `if cancel_check is not None and cancel_check(): raise HttpError("download cancelled by user")` inside the retry-wrapped _do_download
Verifier notes (2)

closeEvent waits on fetch/check threads without quit() — wait always times out, then the running QThread is destroyed (qFatal abort on exit)

app_puller/main_window.py:1526-1547 · reviewer: puller-review
What it is
When the user quits during a pull, closeEvent calls `self._fetch_worker.cancel()` then `self._fetch_thread.wait(3000)` — but never `quit()`. Threads built by `make_thread()` (workers.py:176-187) use the default QThread.run() event loop (`thread.started.connect(worker.run)` runs the worker as a slot inside exec()), so the loop never exits on its own and `wait()` times out even if the worker finished instantly. The same applies to `self._check_thread.wait(2000)`. After the timeout, `event.accept()` lets the app exit; the QThreads are parented to the MainWindow, so window destruction destroys a QThread whose OS thread is still running — Qt's QThread destructor raises qFatal('QThread: Destroyed while thread is still running'), aborting the process. Because cancellation never reaches the in-flight download (see the fetch_one cancel_check finding), the fetch thread is essentially guaranteed to still be running.
Why it matters
Quitting the puller while a pull or a check sweep is running — a completely normal action — freezes the UI for 2-3 seconds and then crashes on exit (macOS 'Python quit unexpectedly'). The manifest save in closeEvent does run first, so data loss is limited, but the crash is user-visible and the in-flight download is killed mid-write leaving a .partial directory consuming disk until the next fetch of that source wipes it.
How to fix
Use the existing `stop_thread()` helper (quit + wait) for the fetch and check threads in closeEvent, exactly as it already does for verify/diagnose two lines below: `self._fetch_worker.cancel(); stop_thread(self._fetch_thread, 3000)`. Once real download cancellation is wired (other finding), the wait will actually succeed; until then, consider a longer timeout plus `thread.terminate()` as last resort, or detach with a warning instead of letting the destructor fire.
Expected improvement
No abort-on-exit when quitting mid-pull; shutdown path matches the verify/diagnose teardown that already does this correctly.
Evidence
if self._fetch_worker:
    self._fetch_worker.cancel()
    self._fetch_thread.wait(3000)
...
if self._check_worker:
    self._check_worker.cancel()
    self._check_thread.wait(2000)
stop_thread(self._verify_thread)
stop_thread(self._diagnose_thread)
highUnverified backend effort: small

Backend owners-index snapshot is never reloaded after a data publish — every sync leaves /api/* serving a different generation than /data/* until a manual restart

backend/app/main.py:116-117 · reviewer: composite-publish-seam
What it is
The backend loads owners_index.json exactly once, at module import: `owners_index = OwnersIndexService(settings.owners_index_path)` (main.py:116-117). OwnersIndexService has a `reload()` method, but grep confirms it is called only from `__init__` (owners_index_service.py:33) — no route, signal, or mtime check ever invokes it again. Meanwhile the browser fetches owners_filter.json, index.json, parcels.pmtiles, and parcels_attr.parquet live from the same volume via Caddy (`handle /data/processed/*`, Caddyfile:31-39), and `deploy.sh data` ends after rclone leg 2 with no restart (deploy.sh:85-102); `deploy.sh all` is worse — it rolls the machine first (`code`), boots the backend against the OLD composite, then syncs new data underneath it (deploy.sh:137-140). The required `fly apps restart` is a manual runbook step (DEPLOYMENT.md:340 'REQUIRED — reload the owners index', and gotcha at lines 371-372). Concrete inconsistency: app/src/lib/owner-cluster.ts computes `display_cluster_id = sha1(normalize_owner_name(name))[:12]` client-side from the freshly synced tiles' parcel_owner, and its own header comment states 'The backend looks a Lead up by exactly this id, so any drift would 404 the Lead page' — an owner newly present in the synced owners_filter.json / pmtiles but absent from the boot-time in-memory index gets 404 'owner not found in index' on GET /api/leads/{cid}, and Candidates counts/scores/distress tiers diverge from the filter sidebar. Nothing detects the skew: both JSONs carry `generated_at` (owners_index.py:589, 662) but no consumer compares them, and /api/health exposes only `owners_indexed` count (main.py:198), not the generation.
Why it matters
This bites on every single data publish in normal operation — the entire period from the end of leg 2 until the operator remembers the manual restart serves owner clusters/scores/tiles from two different generations, producing 404s on Lead pages reachable from the live map and mismatched counts. It is documented as an operator gotcha ('restart to load owners index') instead of being fixed, and the failure is silent: health stays green (an empty or stale index still returns ok:True).
How to fix
Mirror the CredentialsFile pattern already in this codebase (mtime-cached per-request reload of users.txt): have OwnersIndexService stat the file's mtime on access and call reload() when it changes — the producer writes atomically via os.replace (owners_index.py:673-677) and rclone 1.74 renames atomically, so an mtime check is safe. At minimum, append a restart/`supervisorctl restart uvicorn` step to `deploy.sh data --apply` after leg 2, and expose `generated_at` in /api/health so `deploy.sh smoke` can assert the backend's generation matches the volume's owners_filter.json.
Expected improvement
Data publishes become self-contained: no manual restart, no window where the map links 404 against the API, and the 'restart to load owners index' gotcha can be deleted from the runbook.
Evidence
main.py:116-117: "# The owners index is static + shared; load once at import.\nowners_index = OwnersIndexService(settings.owners_index_path)"; DEPLOYMENT.md:340: "fly apps restart morphy-consulting  # REQUIRED — reload the owners index (see gotchas)"; owner-cluster.ts: "any drift would 404 the Lead page"

preprocess.py --compose-only --state never saves the manifest — the compose record is silently dropped

scripts/preprocess.py:264-271 · reviewer: preprocess-quality
What it is
The save policy is inconsistent across the run_* functions: run_normalize_all (runner.py:147) and run_compose_all (runner.py:215) call manifest.save() internally, but run_normalize_one and run_compose_one do not — the caller must save. The CLI compensates for the --source path (preprocess.py:260 `manifest.save()`) but the single-state compose branch returns immediately after run_compose_one (preprocess.py:266-271) without saving. run_compose_one did record_compose() into the in-memory manifest (runner.py:165-185, including the failure entry), so the recorded outcome — timestamp, sources_used, joins_applied, counts, or the 'failed' status + error — is discarded on exit.
Why it matters
After `python scripts/preprocess.py --compose-only --state 12`, the composite files on disk are new but data/processed/preprocess_manifest.json still shows the previous compose (or none). Anything reading the manifest for state — the composed-states summary, failure diagnostics, the rebuilt-vs-live distinction — sees stale information, and a failed single-state compose leaves no recorded error at all. This is exactly the silent-failure mode the manifest exists to prevent.
How to fix
Either add manifest.save() in the --compose-only --state branch before returning (one line, mirrors the --source branch), or — better, removing the whole hazard class — move manifest.save() inside run_compose_one/run_normalize_one (idempotent, atomic via temp+rename already) so all four entry points have the same persistence contract.
Expected improvement
Single-state compose runs leave an accurate manifest; failed composes are diagnosable from the manifest like every other path.
Evidence
preprocess.py:265-271 `if args.state:\n    res = run_compose_one(\n        get_state(args.state),\n        manifest,\n        progress_callback=_stream_progress,\n    )\n    return 0 if res else 1` — no manifest.save(), unlike the --source branch (line 260) and the run_compose_all branch (line 277).
Verifier notes (2)

CONFIRMED, all cited lines accurate: the `--compose-only --state` branch (preprocess.py:264-271) calls run_compose_one and returns without manifest.save(); run_compose_one (runner.py:151-197) calls record_compose into the in-memory manifest on both success (line 177) and failure (line 165) but never persists; record_compose does not auto-save (manifest.py has no internal self.save()); run_normalize_all (runner.py:147) and run_compose_all (runner.py:215) save, and the --source branch saves at preprocess.py:260 — whose help text ("Goes through the manifest like a full run, so the entry stays consistent") shows persistence of single-item CLI runs is the intended contract, so the missing save is an oversight, not a design choice. Only callers of run_compose_one are the buggy branch and run_compose_all. Recovery also exists via --rebuild-manifest (rebuilds composed entries from disk, though it cannot reconstruct a 'failed' entry's error). Net impact: data/processed/preprocess_manifest.json is stale as a human-inspectable record (timestamps, sources_used, joins_applied, counts, failure errors) after a --compose-only --state run — a real defect, but with no automated consequence. All activity dates and 'today' computed as server-UTC date.today() — evening work is logged on the wrong day and tasks go overdue at ~8pm ET

backend/app/lead_store.py:242, 261, 212 (plus backend/app/main.py 411-412, 460, 702) · reviewer: backend-bugs
What it is
Every server-side date stamp uses naive `date.today()`, which in the production container (python:3.11-slim on Fly iad, no TZ set) is the UTC calendar date: auto-logged status rows (`occurred_on=date.today()` in _log_status_change), default attempt dates (`occurred_on or date.today()` in append_attempt — the frontend sends occurred_on='' → null by default, see app/src/lib/lead-attempt.ts), pursue flag rows, the auto-follow-up anchor (`anchor=date.today()` in update_lead), and the overdue computation (`today=date.today()` passed to AttentionService). For a US-based user, anything done after ~7-8pm Eastern is recorded with TOMORROW's date in History/Calendar/Stats, and a task due 'today' flips to overdue in the Attention feed at 7-8pm local instead of midnight.
Why it matters
Dates are the primary axis of the activity read models (History, Calendar, Stats active-days, attention overdue, auto-followup windows). A 2-person founder team working evenings will routinely see off-by-one dates and premature 'overdue' badges, and the auto-followup due date shifts by a day depending on what hour the touch was logged.
How to fix
Pick the product's calendar: either set TZ=America/New_York (or a tenant setting) in fly.toml/Dockerfile and keep date.today(), or compute `datetime.now(ZoneInfo(tenant_tz)).date()` at a single helper used by lead_store/main.py, or have the client send its local date on every write (it already can for attempts; status/flag/dismiss rows currently cannot be dated).
Expected improvement
Activity dates match the user's working day; overdue flips at local midnight; auto-followup due dates stop depending on the hour of day.
Evidence
lead_store.py: `occurred_on=occurred_on or date.today(),` and `_log_status_change`: `occurred_on=date.today(),`; main.py attention route: `today=date.today(),`; main.py update_lead: `maybe_schedule(\n            target, anchor=date.today(), status=\"contacted\"\n        )`
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): Confirmed at every cited location. backend/app/lead_store.py: line 212 (pursue flag row occurred_on=date.today()), line 242 (append_attempt: occurred_on or date.today()), line 261 (_log_status_change: occurred_on=date.today()); backend/app/models.py:84 additionally has default=date.today on the column. backend/app/main.py: lines 411-412 (auto-followup anchor=date.today() on move to Contacted), line 460 (anchor=occurred_on or date.today()), line 702 (today=date.today() into AttentionService). backend/app/attention_service.py:86 computes overdue as due_date < self.today, so a task due today flips overdue when the UTC date rolls (8pm EDT / 7pm EST). app/src/lib/lead-attempt.ts:54-59 confirms the frontend sends occurred_on=null when blank, so the default attempt path uses the server date. fly.toml [env] and Dockerfile set no TZ; runtime is python:3.11-slim on Fly iad, so date.today() is the UTC calendar date — the finding's environment claim is accurate. created_at timestamps are correctly tz-aware UTC (models._utcnow); only the calendar-date fields and 'today' comparisons drift. filter-model.ts: the score-min filter always tests the baked index score while the list/map rank by the active custom scoring config

app/src/lib/filter-model.ts:227 · reviewer: frontend-lib-bugs
What it is
FILTER_FIELDS wires the 'Interestingness score' minimum to the baked owners-index field: `minField('scoreMin', 'score', 0, (o) => o.score)`. Meanwhile, once the user touches a weight or applies a preset (scoring-store, #114/#115), every displayed and ranked score is `effectiveScore` — a custom weighted blend (scoring-store.svelte.ts scoreById feeds explore-towers.svelte.ts, and ExploreList renders `r.score` from it). So under a custom config the slider filters on a quantity the UI no longer shows anywhere: an owner can display 0.9 in the list yet be excluded by 'Minimum: 0.50' because its baked score is 0.3, or vice versa. The scales also diverge — the slider is hard-capped at max=1 (FilterPanel.svelte line 261) which matches the baked 0-1 score but not a custom blend, whose sum-of-weights can exceed 1.
Why it matters
The scoring redesign's stated invariant is that one owner-grained score drives every surface so a preset switch 're-ranks every surface consistently' (scoring.ts header). The score filter is the one consumer that silently forked: with any custom preset active, filtering by score produces results that contradict the scores the user is looking at, which reads as the filter being broken.
How to fix
Make the scoreMin predicate read the same effective score the ranking uses — e.g. have FilterModelStore consult `scoring.scoreOf(o.display_cluster_id)` (or accept an injected scoreOf) in the scoreMin test when `!scoring.useDefault`, and scale the slider bounds off the active score extent. If the divergence is intentional (filter pinned to the baked score by design), rename the control and document it so the two scores are visibly distinct quantities.
Expected improvement
Filtering by minimum score agrees with the scores actually displayed and ranked, under both the default and custom scoring configs.
Evidence
filter-model.ts: minField('scoreMin', 'score', 0, (o) => o.score),

scoring-store.svelte.ts: m.set(o.display_cluster_id, effectiveScore(o, config, ext));
explore-towers.svelte.ts: rankedTowerRows(..., (cid) => scoring.scoreOf(cid), ...)
FilterPanel.svelte:261: type="range" min="0" max="1" step="0.05" value={scoreMinLocal}

compose._load_state_data marks every parcel source with shards on disk as 'used' for every state — confirmed wrong provenance in all four production summaries

scripts/preprocess/compose.py:648-658 · reviewer: preprocess-core
What it is
The tail of _load_state_data appends a parcel normalizer to sources_used whenever its normalized shard directory contains any parquet — with no check that any shard carries rows for the state being composed: `if src_dir.is_dir() and any(src_dir.glob("*.parquet")): sources_used.append(n.source_id)`. I verified the effect on disk: data/processed/composite/05/summary.json (Arkansas) lists 'fdor_nal', 'fl_fgdl_parcels', 'mt_cadastral_parcels' and 'wv_parcels' as sources_used, and the same full cross-state list appears in states 12, 30 and 54. The bad list also flows into build_catalog's `sources_used_versions` lineage block and into manifest.record_compose.
Why it matters
Provenance/lineage for every composed state is wrong: each state's catalog.json claims it was built from all four states' parcel sources, with per-source raw versions attached. Anything that uses sources_used for freshness decisions, debugging ('which raw feed produced this state?'), or the pre-commercial licensing audit (docs/LIMITATIONS.md tracks per-source terms) is misled.
How to fix
Record contributing parcel sources from the chunk loop instead of a directory probe: iter_state_parcel_shards labels are `{source_id}__{shard_stem}`, so collect the source_id prefixes of shards that actually yielded rows for this state (or, in the probe, read just the state_fips column of each shard and check membership).
Expected improvement
summary.json / catalog.json / the manifest carry accurate per-state source lineage.
Evidence
compose.py: `src_dir = NORMALIZED_ROOT / n.source_id / OUTPUT_BASENAMES.get("parcel", "parcel")\n        if src_dir.is_dir() and any(src_dir.glob("*.parquet")):\n            sources_used.append(n.source_id)`; on-disk composite/05/summary.json sources_used: [... 'ar_parcels', 'mt_cadastral_parcels', 'fl_fgdl_parcels', 'fdor_nal', 'wv_parcels']

Recompose cleans parcel shards but leaves stale non-parcel layers and views/*.parquet on disk, which owners_index and the app read by path

scripts/preprocess/compose.py:229-238, 411-424, 451 · reviewer: preprocess-core
What it is
At the top of compose(), only the parcels shard dir and the legacy parcels.parquet are cleaned. Step 8 writes a non-parcel layer only when it has rows (`if table is None or len(table) == 0: continue`), and step 9 writes a view file only when the view produced rows (`if result is None or len(result) == 0: continue`). So if a re-run loses a source (e.g. fcc_asr normalize fails, or a view legitimately drops to zero rows), the previous run's towers.parquet / solar_facilities.parquet / views/<name>.parquet survive untouched. The new catalog.json stops advertising them, but several consumers read by hard-coded path, not via the catalog: owners_index._read_state_towers reads `state_dir / "towers.parquet"` directly, and the freshly-recorded summary.json/catalog can disagree with what's actually on disk under views/.
Why it matters
A recompose that should have removed or emptied a layer instead keeps serving the previous run's data with a fresh-looking directory: the owners index enriches structures (height/active/carriers/lat/lon) from a stale towers.parquet whose carrier and activity data no longer correspond to the current composite, with no warning anywhere.
How to fix
At compose start, alongside the parcel-shard cleanup, delete (or move aside) the known per-type outputs for this state (every OUTPUT_BASENAMES basename's .parquet/.geojson variants) and clear views/*.parquet — the run rewrites everything it owns, so a clean slate makes re-runs idempotent. Alternatively, write a tombstone pass at the end that removes any layer/view file not in output_files.
Expected improvement
Re-running compose over an existing state directory becomes idempotent; owners_index and any path-based reader can never pick up a layer the current run did not produce.
Evidence
compose.py cleanup only covers parcels: `for old in parcels_shard_dir.glob("*.parquet"): old.unlink()` + `legacy_single = out_dir / "parcels.parquet"`; step 8: `if table is None or len(table) == 0:\n            continue`; owners_index.py: `towers = state_dir / "towers.parquet"` (direct path read, not catalog-driven)

NaN-truthy `or` fallback chains on row values silently defeat column fallbacks (uspvdb capacity, hifld asr_number, eia operator/plant-id, hud geoid)

scripts/preprocess/normalizers/uspvdb.py:47-54 · reviewer: preprocess-normalizers
What it is
Several normalizers chain row values with `or` to express "first non-empty column": uspvdb.py:50 `to_float(r.get("p_cap_ac") or r.get("p_cap_dc"))`, uspvdb.py:47/49/54, hifld_towers.py:54-58 (`r.get("TowReg") or r.get("Tower_ID") or r.get("OBJECTID")`), eia_860.py:93, eia_860m.py:66/79, hud_overlays.py:62-68. When the first column EXISTS but holds NaN (the normal pandas missing value), NaN is truthy as a float, so the chain returns NaN instead of falling through — to_float(NaN)→None and safe_str(NaN)→"". Concretely: a USPVDB row with null p_cap_ac but a populated p_cap_dc gets capacity_mw=None; a HIFLD row with null TowReg gets asr_number="" even though Tower_ID/OBJECTID are populated. The codebase's own pandas_coerce.py docstring names this exact trap: "``row.get(k) or ''`` returns NaN unchanged (NaN is truthy as a float)" — but the helpers are applied *after* the `or`, not per-operand.
Why it matters
Fallback columns that were deliberately mapped never fire when the preferred column is null per-row (the common case the fallback exists for). Capacity, IDs and operator names silently come out empty, and the loss is indistinguishable from genuinely-missing source data.
How to fix
Add a small helper (e.g. `first_nonblank(row, *keys)` that applies safe_str/to_float per operand and returns the first non-empty result) in pandas_coerce.py, and replace each `r.get(a) or r.get(b)` chain with it. A unit test with a NaN-in-first-column fixture row pins the behavior.
Expected improvement
Per-row column fallbacks work as the field maps intend; capacity_mw, asr_number, facility_id and operator fields stop silently dropping recoverable values.
Evidence
uspvdb.py:50 `"capacity_mw": to_float(r.get("p_cap_ac") or r.get("p_cap_dc")),`; hifld_towers.py:54-56 `safe_str(r.get("TowReg") or r.get("Tower_ID") or r.get("OBJECTID"))`; pandas_coerce.py:5-7 "``row.get(k) or ''`` returns NaN unchanged (NaN is truthy as a float)"
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): Confirmed in current code. Read all five normalizers + pandas_coerce.py. backend/app/lead_store.py:105-123 · reviewer: backend-quality
What it is
Two independent derivations of 'the antenna that sparked pursuit' exist. LeadStore.latest_flags() builds `{display_cluster_id -> ASR}` keyed by the raw cid each flag row was written under, and owners_index_service.list_my_leads (line 215) looks it up by the *effective* (post-merge) root: `flags.get(cid, "")`. lead_view() instead concatenates attempts across all merged members and takes the first flag (line 266). Why it matters
The same lead shows a trigger antenna on one surface and none on another after a merge — a visible inconsistency, and a soft violation of the backend AGENTS.md invariant that merge resolution must never be forked per surface. It is also classic duplicated-derivation drift: two code paths answer one question differently.
How to fix
Resolve flags through the merge map in one place: in list_my_leads, build the flag dict as `{merges.resolve(cid): asr}` (latest-write-wins, which the oldest-to-newest iteration in latest_flags already provides), or move the 'trigger of an effective owner' computation into a single shared helper used by both list_my_leads and lead_view.

Manifest is mutated concurrently from worker threads and the UI thread with no lock — workers.py's 'no shared mutable state' claim is false

app_puller/workers.py:4-5, 27-31, 80-84 · reviewer: puller-review
What it is
workers.py's module docstring says 'Workers communicate with the UI exclusively via Qt signals — no shared mutable state', but both CheckWorker and FetchWorker hold `self._manifest` (the same Manifest object the main window uses) and mutate it off the UI thread: CheckWorker runs `check_one(s, self._manifest)` across an 8-thread pool (each call does `manifest.mark_check`/`mark_check_failed`/`update_cadence`), FetchWorker calls `manifest.mark_fetch_ok/_failed` and `self._manifest.save()` per source. Meanwhile the UI thread keeps reading and writing the same object: `_refresh_header` runs on a 5 s QTimer, and 'Rescan disk' (`btn_rescan`) and the attention-panel 'Walk me through' button are NOT disabled by `_set_buttons_busy`, so `_detect_manual_installs()` and `mark_manual_install()` can mutate entries and call `manifest.save()` on the UI thread while a fetch worker is doing the same on its thread. `Manifest.save()` itself is atomic on disk (mkstemp + os.replace), but `asdict(entry)` can serialize an entry mid-update (e.g. `mark_fetch_ok` sets local_version/fetched_at/bytes/files one field at a time), persisting a torn snapshot, and concurrent rescans can clobber a fetch's in-progress entry state.
Why it matters
Under the GIL this mostly 'works', but it is exactly the silent-failure-mode fragility class: a rescan or walkthrough completed during an active pull can write a manifest where an entry has the new local_version but stale bytes/files, or overwrite the worker's status transition. The puller's own contract ('the manifest is the single writer', manifest.py docstring) is violated within a single process.
How to fix
Either (a) add a `threading.Lock` to Manifest, taken in entry-mutating methods and around the snapshot built in `save()`, or (b) keep all manifest writes on one thread by having workers emit outcomes and letting the main thread apply mark_* + save. Additionally disable `btn_rescan` (and attention-panel actions) in `_set_buttons_busy` while a fetch/check is running.
Expected improvement
No torn manifest snapshots on disk, no lost status transitions when the user rescans or completes a walkthrough during a pull; the docstring contract becomes true.
Evidence
"""...Workers communicate with the UI
exclusively via Qt signals — no shared mutable state."""
...
    def __init__(self, sources: list[DataSource], manifest: Manifest) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self._sources = sources
        self._manifest = manifest
...
                self._manifest.save()

app_puller/main_window.py:764-785, 910-912, 1159-1165 · reviewer: puller-review
What it is
`_set_buttons_busy(True)` disables the four top action buttons plus btn_action/btn_upload, but two paths re-enable or bypass them while a check sweep runs: (1) `_show_source()` gates buttons only on `self._fetch_thread is None` — selecting any row (or a check completing for the selected row, which calls `_show_source`) re-enables Pull mid-check; (2) the attention-panel '↻ Refresh' buttons are never disabled and call `_start_fetch([s])`, which only guards `if self._fetch_thread is not None`, not the check thread. Once a fetch is running concurrently with the check, `_on_check_all_done` fires `_set_buttons_busy(False)` and `self.pb_label.setText("Idle. No pull in progress.")` while the download is mid-flight, and the two flows interleave writes to the shared `pb_label`. `btn_preprocess` and `btn_verify` are also left enabled during pulls, allowing preprocess to read a source's current/ during the promote_partial rename window.
Why it matters
This is the entry point for the cross-thread manifest races (both workers mutate the same Manifest), and it leaves the UI lying about state: progress label says Idle during an active download, pull buttons enabled when a pull can't actually be started (clicking just produces the 'Already running' warning), and check-progress text overwrites fetch-progress text.

Expected improvement
Exactly one background flow at a time (matching the single-track progress-bar design stated in FetchWorker's docstring), and the progress panel always reflects reality.
Evidence
self.btn_action.setEnabled(self._fetch_thread is None)

    def _start_fetch(self, sources: list[DataSource]) -> None:

        self._set_buttons_busy(False)

users.py read-modify-write has no file lock — concurrent password changes / CLI edits silently lose updates

backend/app/users.py:80-109, 148-161 · reviewer: credentials-file-runtime-lifecycle
What it is
Every mutation in users.py (add_user, remove_user, set_password, set_team, change_password) is an unlocked read-whole-file → modify-lines → whole-file os.replace. Verified: no flock/fcntl/lockfile exists anywhere in backend/app/ (grep finds only login_throttle's threading.Lock). The route `def change_my_password` (main.py:315) is sync, so FastAPI runs it in the AnyIO threadpool — two concurrent POST /api/me/password requests genuinely run in parallel threads, and an operator running `python -m app.users` over `fly ssh console` (the documented Day-2 flow) races the live route in a separate process, which a threading.Lock alone could not fix. The race window is wide: set_password computes hash_password(password) — 240,000 PBKDF2 iterations, on the order of 100ms+ on Fly's shared-cpu-1x — *between* its _read_lines (line 84) and _write_atomic (line 88). Last writer wins: the loser's hash or team change is reverted while their request still returned 204/success. The change_password verify-then-set_password double-read narrows the lost-update window for OTHER lines (set_password re-reads fresh state), but adds a TOCTOU on the user's own line: the current password is verified against the hash from the first read (line 106), then set_password unconditionally overwrites whatever hash the second read found — so an operator `set-password` landing between the two reads is verified-against-stale and then clobbered.
Why it matters
A user whose password change is silently reverted got a 204 and now cannot log in with the password they believe they set — and because the file re-authorizes every request, a lost set-team or remove-user edit is an authorization change that silently never happened. With a 2-person team the probability is low per-day, but the failure is silent, security-relevant, and will be impossible to diagnose after the fact (the file shows no trace of the lost write).
How to fix
Wrap the read-modify-write in an advisory file lock that both the route and the CLI take, e.g. `fcntl.flock` on a sibling lockfile (`users.txt.lock`) held from _read_lines through os.replace — it works across processes on the same machine, which covers both the threadpool and the fly-ssh CLI since prod is a single machine by design. Independently, hoist `hash_password(...)` above `_read_lines` in set_password so the expensive KDF no longer sits inside the critical window.
Expected improvement
Concurrent credential mutations serialize instead of silently reverting each other; the verify/overwrite TOCTOU in change_password closes; the race window shrinks from ~100ms+ of CPU to microseconds of file I/O.
Evidence
users.py:84-88: `lines = _read_lines(file)` ... `lines[idx] = f"{entry.username}:{hash_password(password)}:{entry.team}"` ... `_write_atomic(file, lines)` (no lock; password.py: `DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 240_000`). main.py:315: `def change_my_password(` (sync → threadpool). users.py:106-108: `if not verify_password(current, entry.encoded_hash): return ... WRONG_CURRENT` then `set_password(file, username, new)` re-reads and overwrites unconditionally.
mediumUnverified deploy effort: medium

Live range-read sessions straddle mid-sync file replacement — DuckDB-wasm keeps stale parquet offsets with no ETag validation; pmtiles self-heals only if Caddy emits strong ETags

app/src/lib/duckdb.ts:108-117 · reviewer: composite-publish-seam
What it is
Caddy's file_server opens the file fresh on every HTTP range request, and rclone 1.74's local backend replaces each file via `.partial` + atomic rename (the Dockerfile pins `rclone/rclone:1.74`, Dockerfile:31) — so no single request reads torn bytes, but a multi-request read session spans the swap. duckdb-wasm registers each multi-MB parquet by URL (`db.registerFileURL(REGISTERED_FILE, absolute, duckdb.DuckDBDataProtocol.HTTP, false)`, duckdb.ts:115) and a `parquet_scan` issues many independent range requests (footer, then row groups, lazily across later queries); duckdb-wasm's HTTP filesystem sends no If-Range/ETag validator, so once rclone renames a new parcels_attr.parquet or towers.parquet into place, an open tab applies generation-N footer offsets to generation-N+1 bytes — decode errors or wrong rows until a full page reload. The pmtiles path is partially protected: pmtiles v4's FetchSource records the ETag and throws EtagMismatch when a later response's ETag differs, invalidating and refetching the header (verified in app/node_modules/pmtiles/dist/pmtiles.js) — but it explicitly discards weak ETags (`h.startsWith("W/")&&(h=null)`), and the Caddyfile enables `encode gzip` site-wide (Caddyfile:20), which weakens ETags on encoded responses; the protection therefore rests on 206 responses bypassing the encoder, which is unverified in this config and untested (tests/test_caddy_serving.py doesn't assert ETag strength).
Why it matters
The co-founder triaging in an open tab during (or after) a data publish gets corrupt parcel attribute reads — DuckDB query failures or silently wrong owner/score rows — with no error pathway that tells him to reload. Caddy's `Cache-Control: private, no-store` on /data/processed/* protects across page loads but does nothing for in-session readers.
How to fix
The versioned-directory publish (previous finding) eliminates this class outright — per-generation URLs are immutable so an open session keeps reading its own generation. Standalone mitigations: catch DuckDB read errors in duckdb.ts and re-register the file URL (forcing a fresh footer read), and add an assertion to tests/test_caddy_serving.py that range responses on .pmtiles/.parquet carry strong (non-W/) ETags so the pmtiles EtagMismatch self-heal is actually armed.
Expected improvement
Open sessions survive a data publish instead of degrading into decode errors or silently wrong rows; the pmtiles ETag self-heal is verified rather than assumed.
Evidence
duckdb.ts:115: "await db.registerFileURL(REGISTERED_FILE, absolute, duckdb.DuckDBDataProtocol.HTTP, false);"; pmtiles.js (v4 FetchSource): "let h=c.headers.get(\"Etag\");h!=null&&h.startsWith(\"W/\")&&(h=null);...if(c.status===416||i&&h&&h!==i)throw this.mustReload=!0,new B(...)"
lowConfirmed backend effort: trivial

Stats reply-rate and state-filtered metrics drop pre-merge activity: attempts keep raw cids while leads use effective (merged) cids

backend/app/main.py:842-850 (consumed at backend/app/stats_service.py 129-147) · reviewer: backend-bugs
What it is
The /api/stats route builds LeadRow from the merge-resolved effective owners (`owners_index.effective(merges)` — cids are post-merge display ids) but builds ActivityRow with the raw persisted cid (`cid=a.display_cluster_id` from store.tenant_attempts()). StatsService then intersects the two cid spaces: `_reply_rate` computes `replied`/`outbound` sets from attempt cids and intersects with lead cids, and the state filter builds `state_cids` from leads and drops attempts whose cid isn't in it. Any touch or reply logged on a cluster before it was merged away no longer matches the surviving lead's cid, so it silently disappears from reply rate, from state-scoped volume/active-days, and from the outbound-implies-contacted rule.
Why it matters
The Dashboard stats quietly understate reply rate and state-filtered activity exactly for the owners the user manually curated (merged) — the leads most likely to be actively worked. The error grows with every merge and is invisible (no error, just wrong numbers).
How to fix
Resolve each attempt's cid through the request's merge service when assembling ActivityRow in the route: `cid=merges.resolve(a.display_cluster_id)` (merges is already a dependency of the stats route, and OwnerMergeService.override_map is instance-cached so this is O(1) per row).
Expected improvement
Reply rate, state-filtered volume, and contacted-derivation count pre-merge activity against the surviving lead, matching what the Lead page shows (lead_view already concatenates member attempt streams).
Evidence
main.py: `ActivityRow(\n            occurred_on=a.occurred_on, ... cid=a.display_cluster_id,` vs `owners, _ = owners_index.effective(merges)`; stats_service.py: `replied = {a.cid for a in acts if a.direction == \"inbound\"}` ... `return round(len(replied & contacted) / len(contacted), 4)`
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): Confirmed. main.py stats route: leads built from owners_index.effective(merges) (line 828, post-merge cids) while ActivityRow uses raw persisted cid (line 846, cid=a.display_cluster_id); OwnerMergeService docstring explicitly says attempts are never migrated on merge and read paths must reconcile — the sibling /api/activity route does (merges.resolve at main.py:898) but /api/stats does not, violating the 'resolution is never forked per surface' invariant in OwnersIndexService.effective's docstring (so not intentional-by-design). stats_service.py _reply_rate (129-147) intersects the two cid spaces, dropping pre-merge inbound/outbound touches from reply rate; the state filter is at stats_service.py:75-87 (correction to the cited 129-147) and drops pre-merge attempts from volume_by_channel/activity_total/active_days/by_member when state is set. No mitigation found: no attempt-row rewrite on merge, no test coverage of merge+stats (grepped test_stats_service.py, test_api_dashboard.py). Not a duplicate of the 2026-06-07 perf audit — PERF-017 touches the same route but concerns LeadRow allocation volume, not cid resolution. Minor corrections: resolve is O(chain length) not strictly O(1); unfiltered volume/active_days are unaffected (consistent with the finding's wording). update_lead schedules an auto follow-up whenever body.status == 'contacted', even when no transition occurred

backend/app/main.py:409-413 · reviewer: backend-bugs
What it is
The auto-followup trigger in update_lead is `if body.status == "contacted"` — it fires on the request payload, not on an actual transition. LeadStore.upsert no-ops on a same-status save (`if status is not None and status != lead.status`), so a redundant PUT with status='contacted' (stale second tab, retry, or any client that sends the full object) re-runs maybe_schedule with anchor=today. If the previous auto chase was already completed (status done — has_open checks `Task.status != DONE_STATUS`), a brand-new 'Follow up:' task is created with a fresh window even though no new outreach happened. The current LeadPage guards same-status sends client-side (`if (!l || s === l.status) return;`), so the contract relies on every client behaving.
Why it matters
Spurious auto tasks accumulate in the unified Tasks list/Calendar/Attention feed, and the rule's documented semantics ('first outbound touch or status → Contacted eagerly creates') are enforced only by frontend discipline rather than the API.
How to fix
Have LeadStore.upsert return (or expose) whether a status transition actually happened (e.g. compare lead.status before/after, or return the old status) and gate maybe_schedule on a real new→contacted/researching→contacted transition.
Expected improvement
Auto chases are created exactly once per genuine move to Contacted regardless of client behavior, retries, or concurrent tabs.
Evidence
main.py: `if body.status == \"contacted\":\n        _auto_followup(store, db, merges).maybe_schedule(\n            target, anchor=date.today(), status=\"contacted\"\n        )`; lead_store.py upsert: `if status is not None and status != lead.status:` (same-status saves skip the transition entirely)
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): Confirmed in current code. backend/app/main.py:410-413 gates the auto-followup on `body.status == "contacted"` (request payload), not on an actual transition; lead_store.py:174 (`if status is not None and status != lead.status:`) silently no-ops a same-status save without raising, so the route reaches maybe_schedule anyway; auto_followup_rule.py maybe_schedule (lines 43-64) has no transition awareness — its guards are enabled-flag, awaiting-status, _has_open_auto (task_store.py has_open filters Task.status != DONE_STATUS, so a completed prior chase does not block), and _has_reply_since anchored at today. Net effect exactly as described: a redundant PUT with status='contacted' after the prior auto chase is done creates a fresh 'Follow up:' task with a new window despite no new outreach. Frontend guard confirmed at app/src/components/LeadPage.svelte:141 (`if (!l || s === l.status) return;`) — enforcement is client-side only. Minor corrections: the `if` statement is at line 410 (409 is a comment line, cited range 409-413 is fine); an immediate retry is actually blocked by _has_open_auto while the first task is still open — the spurious case requires the prior task to be done or absent, which the finding itself states correctly. Not a duplicate of the 2026-06-07 perf audit: PERF-023 touched this code path only for SQL-filter/index efficiency (and appears already executed via has_open). Rule docstring and unit tests (backend/tests/test_auto_followup_rule.py) describe/cover transition-event semantics, so this is a genuine gap, not intended design. Severity low is right; suggested fix (upsert exposing whether a transition occurred) is sound.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): Verified in backend/app/main.py:409-413 — the trigger is literally `if body.status == "contacted":` on the request payload, after `store.upsert(...)`, with no knowledge of whether a transition occurred. backend/app/lead_store.py:174 confirms `if status is not None and status != lead.status:` — a same-status PUT skips validate_transition, skips the auto-logged status row, and raises nothing (so no 409 shields the trigger). backend/app/auto_followup_rule.py:43-64 confirms the only guards are: rule enabled, status in {new,researching,contacted}, `_has_open_auto` (TaskStore.has_open filters `Task.status != DONE_STATUS`, task_store.py:78-95), and `_has_reply_since(cid, anchor)` with anchor=date.today() — which only blocks replies occurring today or later. Task label filter substring-matches across the comma-joined labels column — 'dev' matches 'devops'

backend/app/task_store.py:240-242 · reviewer: backend-bugs
What it is
list_filtered implements the label filter as `Task.labels.ilike(f"%{normalize_labels(label)}%")` over the normalized comma-separated string. Because there are no boundary anchors, filtering by label 'dev' also returns tasks labeled 'devops' or 'frontend-dev', and filtering by 'out' matches 'outreach'. Additionally, `%`/`_` in the user-supplied label (and in the free-text `q`, lines 246-255) act as LIKE wildcards rather than literals.
Why it matters
The Tasks filter (#110) silently returns wrong rows whenever one label is a substring of another — a likely shape given the documented examples ('dev,outreach') — and the user has no signal that the result set is over-inclusive.
How to fix
Match with delimiters: `(',' || labels || ',') LIKE '%,' || :label || ',%'` (works on SQLite and Postgres), or fetch and filter exact membership in Python via label_list(); escape %/_ in label and q with an ESCAPE clause if keeping LIKE.
Expected improvement
Label filtering returns exactly the tasks carrying that label; wildcard characters in filter inputs behave literally.
Evidence
task_store.py: `# comma-separated store → substring match on the normalized label.\n            stmt = stmt.where(Task.labels.ilike(f\"%{normalize_labels(label)}%\"))`
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): Confirmed at /Users/danielgentile/Projects/pilot/backend/app/task_store.py lines 240-242: `stmt = stmt.where(Task.labels.ilike(f"%{normalize_labels(label)}%"))` — an unanchored ILIKE substring match over the comma-joined labels column, exactly as the evidence quotes (comment included). `normalize_labels` (lines 40-51) only trims/lowercases/dedupes; it does not escape `%`/`_`, so LIKE wildcards in a user-supplied label pass through. Same for free-text `q`: the block is actually lines 245-256 (finding said 246-255 — off by one, immaterial), `like = f"%{q.strip().lower()}%"` with no ESCAPE clause. The route wiring is real: GET /api/tasks?label=... in backend/app/main.py:589-615 passes label straight into list_filtered, so 'dev' matching 'devops' is reachable on the API surface. Aggravating detail the finding missed: the docstring (line 222) claims list_filtered "Mirrors the client task-filter registry's semantics", but the client (app/src/lib/task-filter.ts:127) does EXACT membership — `t.labels.includes(label)` where labels is string[] (tasks-api.ts:20) — so the server demonstrably diverges from its own stated contract. One mitigating correction to "why_it_matters": the shipped Tasks UI does not currently consume the server-side label param — tasks-store.svelte.ts:45 filters locally via matchesTask (exact membership), and taskQueryString has no consumer in app/src outside the lib/tests — so today's UI masks the over-match; it bites direct API callers and any future switch to server-side filtering. That fits the assigned 'low' severity. Not a duplicate of the 2026-06-07 perf audit (grep of docs/PERF_AUDIT_2026-06-07.md shows only unrelated 'label' mentions; this is a correctness bug, not a perf finding). Proposed fix (delimiter-anchored LIKE or exact membership in Python via label_list, plus ESCAPE for %/_) is sound.

Check-then-act find-or-create/upsert paths (LeadStore, settings stores, AntennaStore, OwnerMergeService) race to IntegrityError 500s; concurrent merges can commit a persistent override cycle

backend/app/lead_store.py:140-151 (also owner_merge_service.py 104-118, settings_store.py 42-49, user_settings_store.py 72-79, antenna_store.py 40-46) · reviewer: backend-bugs
What it is
Several first-write paths do SELECT-then-INSERT on composite-PK tables with no IntegrityError handling: LeadStore.get_or_create, AntennaStore.get_or_create, SettingsStore.set, UserSettingsStore.set, and OwnerMergeService.merge. Prod runs one uvicorn worker but sync routes execute on a ~40-thread pool, so a double-submit (two tabs, retry, two teammates touching the same lead) can have both threads pass the existence check and one fail the PK insert with an unhandled IntegrityError → 500 with the session left dirty. TenantService.find_or_create (tenant_service.py 38-46) already demonstrates the correct catch-rollback-reread pattern. OwnerMergeService.merge has a worse variant: two concurrent merges (A→B and B→A) each resolve against a stale override_map, pass the `src_root == tgt_root` cycle guard, and commit a two-row cycle; resolve()'s seen-set then returns each id to itself, so the merge silently vanishes — and re-merging cannot repair it (merge re-points A→B to the same target, leaving the cycle in place; only a split clears it).
Why it matters
Plain 500s on benign double-clicks, and in the merge case a self-inconsistent override state that persists in the DB with no UI-visible cause or obvious recovery.
How to fix
Wrap the INSERT in each get_or_create/set in a try/except IntegrityError with rollback + re-read (copy TenantService.find_or_create). For merge, additionally re-check the resolved roots after acquiring the row (or detect a cycle post-commit and delete the newer edge); a CHECK that source != target plus a post-resolve assertion would catch the 2-cycle.
Expected improvement
Concurrent first-writes degrade to a harmless lost-update instead of a 500, and override state can never reach a resolve()-degenerate cycle.
Evidence
lead_store.py: `lead = self.get_lead(display_cluster_id)\n        if lead is None:\n            lead = Lead(...)\n            self.session.add(lead)\n            self.session.flush()`; owner_merge_service.py: `src_root = self.resolve(source)\n        tgt_root = self.resolve(target)\n        if src_root == tgt_root:\n            raise MergeError(...)` (stale instance-cached `_map` between the two requests); contrast tenant_service.py: `except IntegrityError:\n                self.session.rollback()\n                tenant = self.get_by_name(name)`
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): Read all six cited files. Confirmed: (1) LeadStore.get_or_create (lead_store.py:140-151), AntennaStore.get_or_create (antenna_store.py:40-46), SettingsStore.set (settings_store.py:42-49), UserSettingsStore.set (user_settings_store.py:72-79) all do SELECT-then-INSERT on composite-PK tables (PKs verified in models.py) with zero IntegrityError handling — the only IntegrityError catch in the backend is TenantService.find_or_create (tenant_service.py:33-48), which matches the finding's cited correct pattern verbatim. (2) Concurrency premise holds: every main.py route is sync `def` (anyio threadpool), get_db (db.py:137-143) is request-scoped, SQLite engine uses check_same_thread=False — concurrent threads can both pass the existence check. (3) OwnerMergeService.merge cycle scenario verified by hand-trace: concurrent A→B and B→A each load a per-instance _map before either commits, both pass the src_root==tgt_root guard (lines 103-106, finding cites 104-118 — off by one), and commit a persistent 2-row cycle; resolve()'s seen-set then returns each id to itself so the merge silently disappears; re-merging re-points the existing edge to the same target and cannot repair it — only split deletes a row. All exactly as claimed. Corrections: on SQLite (prod backend) the second writer could alternatively surface OperationalError 'database is locked', though with sqlite3's 5s busy timeout IntegrityError post-commit is the likely outcome — unhandled 500 either way; 'session left dirty' is overstated since get_db's finally close() implicitly rolls back the request-scoped session. Not a duplicate of the 2026-06-07 perf audit (read-performance only; PERF-009/022 touch the same files for unrelated reasons; no concurrency/IntegrityError finding among the 42). Severity low is appropriate: 2-person team makes the windows narrow and the get_or_create 500s retry-recoverable; the merge cycle is the nastiest variant but needs two opposite concurrent merges.
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): Read all six cited files plus backend/app/main.py, backend/app/db.py, backend/app/models.py, docs/PERF_AUDIT_2026-06-07.md (grep), and backend/tests/test_teams.py. Every claim checks out. (1) Code sites confirmed at the stated lines: LeadStore.get_or_create (lead_store.py:140-151), AntennaStore.get_or_create (antenna_store.py:40-46), SettingsStore.set (settings_store.py:42-49), UserSettingsStore.set (user_settings_store.py:72-79), OwnerMergeService.merge (owner_merge_service.py:103-118) — all SELECT-then-INSERT on composite-PK tables (PKs confirmed in models.py: lead (tenant_id, display_cluster_id); owner_cluster_override (tenant_id, source_cluster_id); antenna_override (tenant_id, asr); tenant_setting (tenant_id, key); user_setting (tenant_id, user_id, key)) with zero IntegrityError handling — a repo-wide grep shows tenant_service.py:40 is the ONLY IntegrityError catch in the backend. (3) The merge 2-cycle is real: I traced it — each request's OwnerMergeService has its own instance-cached _map (get_merges in main.py creates one per request); concurrent A→B and B→A both load empty maps, both pass the src_root==tgt_root guard, and the two INSERTs hit different PKs (tenant,A) and (tenant,B) so BOTH commit. resolve()'s seen-set then returns each id to itself (merge silently vanishes), and re-merging is a no-op (existing (tenant,A) row re-pointed to the same B), so only split() clears it — exactly as described. Materiality check on severity: 2-person team, millisecond window, and the get_or_create paths self-heal on retry (the row exists afterward), so "low" is the right severity; the merge-cycle variant is vanishingly unlikely (two opposite merges in the same window) but its consequence is persistent silent state corruption with no UI-visible cause, which justifies keeping it at low rather than info. The proposed fix (copy tenant_service.py's catch-rollback-reread; post-resolve re-check or source!=target CHECK plus cycle detection for merge) is correct, minimal, and matches the codebase's own established pattern.

activity-store.svelte.ts: filter-driven load() has no staleness guard, so out-of-order responses can render results for the wrong filter

app/src/lib/activity-store.svelte.ts:61-86 · reviewer: frontend-lib-bugs
What it is
Every setFilter()/resetFilter() fires `void this.load()`, and load() unconditionally writes whatever response arrives into `events`/`lastContactOn`/`status`. There is no request token or abort: two quick filter changes (e.g. picking a member then a channel, or editing the two date inputs back-to-back) issue two overlapping requests, and if the first (now stale) response resolves last, it overwrites the newer one — the History/Calendar views then show events that do not match the currently-selected filter until the user touches a control again. The codebase already has the canonical fix in filter-engine.ts (a monotonic `#token` with `#emit` discarding superseded results), so this store regressed against an established local pattern.
Why it matters
Silent wrong-data display: the filter chips say one thing and the event list shows another. It is intermittent and network-timing dependent, which makes it look like flaky data rather than a client race.
How to fix
Add a monotonic request token (the filter-engine.ts pattern): capture `const token = ++this.#token` at the top of load() and discard the response if the token is no longer current; or pass an AbortController and cancel the in-flight request in setFilter().
Expected improvement
The activity views always reflect the active filter regardless of response ordering.
Evidence
async load(): Promise<void> {
    this.status = 'loading';
    try {
      const qs = queryString(this.filter);
      const res = await request<ActivityResponse>(`/activity${qs ? `?${qs}` : ''}`);
      this.events = res.events;
...
  setFilter(patch: Partial<ActivityFilter>): void {
    this.filter = { ...this.filter, ...patch };
    void this.load();
  }
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): Confirmed in current code. Read app/src/lib/activity-store.svelte.ts in full: load() (lines 61-77) unconditionally assigns the response into $state and sets status='ready'; setFilter (79-82) and resetFilter (83-86) each fire `void this.load()` with no token, debounce, or abort — so a stale response resolving after a newer one overwrites it while the filter chips show the newer selection. Checked for mitigations and found none: History.svelte wires 4 onchange filter controls + a Clear button straight to setFilter/resetFilter with controls never disabled during loading; Calendar/Dashboard also call load() on mount; leads-api.ts request() has no AbortController/signal support; no unit or e2e test covers the store (stale e2e posture, #92). The cited canonical fix is real and local: filter-engine.ts implements exactly the monotonic #token (line 73, ++ at 89) + #emit discard (`if (token !== this.#token) return;`, line 120) pattern, so the proposed fix is correct and convention-consistent. Not a duplicate of the 2026-06-07 perf audit — that audit's /api/activity findings (PERF-006/008/009) are backend cost issues; no frontend staleness finding exists among its 42 items. Corrections: minor only — race window is one request latency since controls fire on onchange commit (not per keystroke), so it needs two quick changes back-to-back; intermittent and self-correcting on the next interaction, which supports keeping severity at low. Line range 61-86 accurate. channelVocab surviving stale responses is intentional (never-shrinks union, lines 54-57) and correctly excluded from the finding.

leads-store.svelte.ts: moveLead() reverts via an array index captured before the await, which can corrupt a different card

app/src/lib/leads-store.svelte.ts:102-122 · reviewer: frontend-lib-bugs
What it is
moveLead() captures `const i = this.myLeads.findIndex(...)` once, applies the optimistic `this.myLeads[i].status = to`, then awaits updateLead(). The rejection path runs `this.myLeads[i].status = from` using the same index — but `this.myLeads` can have been replaced during the await (loadMyLeads() runs on every Leads-surface mount and assigns a fresh array). If the array was reloaded or reordered, index `i` now addresses a different owner, so the revert stamps the dragged card's old status onto an unrelated lead (local UI only; the server is unaffected). If the array shrank below `i`, `this.myLeads[i]` is undefined and the `.status` write throws a TypeError from inside the catch block, escaping moveLead entirely.
Why it matters
A rejected drag (409 illegal transition, or a network blip) that races a worklist refresh visually mislabels a different lead's kanban lane until the next reload — confusing on the surface whose whole job is trusted status state.
How to fix
Re-resolve the card by id at revert time: `const card = this.myLeads.find((o) => o.display_cluster_id === displayClusterId); if (card) card.status = from;` (and use the same find for the optimistic write so both halves address the object, not the index).
Expected improvement
A failed drag always snaps back exactly the card that was dragged, even when the worklist refreshed mid-flight.
Evidence
const i = this.myLeads.findIndex((o) => o.display_cluster_id === displayClusterId);
...
    this.myLeads[i].status = to; // optimistic — the card jumps lanes now
    try {

...
    } catch (e) {
      this.myLeads[i].status = from; // server said no → snap back

App.svelte/url.ts: the 'deep link wins over personal filter default' guard checks a retired ?filters= param the app never writes

app/src/App.svelte:98-105 · reviewer: frontend-lib-bugs
What it is
bootUserSettings() decides whether to apply the saved personal filter default with `readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null`, i.e. by looking for a `?filters=` query param in the legacy parcel-FilterState shape (url.ts). But nothing in the app writes that param anymore: the URL-sync write half (buildUrl/encodeFilters) has no callers, there is no copy-link/share feature, and Explore filter state (the FilterModel) has no URL serialization at all — grep confirms `readUrlState` at App.svelte:101 is the only consumer and no `replaceState`/`pushState`/`filters=` writer exists. So `urlHasFilters` is always false in practice, and the documented behavior "a shared deep link carrying its own filters always wins" (shouldApplyFilterDefault, filter-model.ts lines 301-306) is dead: it can only ever trigger on a hand-crafted URL in a format the current filter system doesn't even read. If FilterModel deep links are added later under a different param/hash format, this guard will silently not see them and the personal default will clobber the shared link's filters.
Why it matters
The guard creates a false sense that the deep-link-wins contract is implemented and tested, when the protected scenario cannot occur; the first person to add real filter deep links will inherit a subtle clobbering bug behind a check that looks like it already handles it.
How to fix
Either implement FilterModel URL serialization (the `serializeFilterModel` snapshot format already mirrors what a `filters` param would carry) and point the guard at that param, or replace the check with an explicit `false` plus a comment stating no filter deep links exist yet — and retire url.ts's write half alongside the rest of the dead parcel-filter path.
Expected improvement
The deep-link-vs-default precedence is either real and verifiable or visibly absent, instead of a vestigial check against a format the app no longer produces.
Evidence
App.svelte:101: const urlHasFilters = readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null;
filter-model.ts: export function shouldApplyFilterDefault(urlHasFilters: boolean, applyPref: boolean): boolean {
  return applyPref && !urlHasFilters;
}
(grep: no occurrence of `replaceState`, `pushState`, or a `filters=` writer anywhere in app/src)
Verifier notes (2)
  • CONFIRM (impact lens, high confidence): Read app/src/App.svelte:80-140, app/src/lib/url.ts (whole file), app/src/lib/filter-model.ts:290-320, app/tests/unit/url.test.ts and filter-model.test.ts grep hits, app/tests/e2e/url.spec.ts, and the PERF-030 section + coverage notes of docs/PERF_AUDIT_2026-06-07.md plus the c47e184 commit message. Confirmed: (1) App.svelte:101 (inside bootUserSettings, lines 98-105) is the only runtime consumer of readUrlState/url.ts; buildUrl/encodeFilters have zero runtime callers — only unit tests (app/tests/unit/url.test.ts). (2) No pushState/replaceState/location.search writers exist anywhere in app/src; all navigation is hash-only (location.hash), and the other URLSearchParams uses (Stats.svelte, task-filter.ts, activity-store) build API query strings, not browser URLs. So urlHasFilters is always false for any app-generated URL and the deep-link-wins branch of shouldApplyFilterDefault (filter-model.ts:304-306; doc comment 301-303 — finding's line cite is accurate) is unreachable in practice. (3) Stronger than the finding states: even on a hand-crafted ?filters= URL, decodeFilters yields the legacy parcel FilterState and nothing applies it to the FilterModel — so the guard would suppress the personal default while applying nothing, leaving the user unfiltered; the contract is half-implemented, not just unexercised. (4) The historical writer was the pre-redesign parcel FilterPanel; a stale e2e spec (app/tests/e2e/url.spec.ts, 'applying a filter writes it to the URL') still tests that retired sync, but the e2e suite is the known-stale #92 suite, not a mitigation. NOT a duplicate of the perf audit: PERF-030 covers url.ts only as dead-code bundle weight, was drafted before the Settings PR (b6389be) added this very import, and was explicitly DEFERRED in c47e184 ('premise stale — modules are test-live') — this finding is a distinct correctness-lens issue about the bootUserSettings guard that postdates the audit's premise. Note also that this App.svelte import is what now keeps url.ts runtime-live and blocks the PERF-030 cleanup. Severity low is right: no reachable user harm today (the default applies when opted in, which is correct in every reachable scenario); the harm is latent (future FilterModel deep links under a different param/hash format will be silently clobbered behind a guard + unit tests at filter-model.test.ts:252-255 that look like coverage). Both proposed fixes are sound; the explicit-false-plus-comment option is the cheaper one and also unblocks deleting url.ts per PERF-030.
  • CONFIRM (existence lens, high confidence): Confirmed at app/src/App.svelte:98-105 and app/src/lib/filter-model.ts:301-306, exactly as cited. The guard `readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null` (App.svelte:101) checks a `?filters=` query param in the legacy parcel FilterState shape (url.ts decodeFilters merges onto emptyFilters from ./filters). Grep-verified: readUrlState's only consumer is App.svelte:101; buildUrl/encodeFilters have no callers outside url.ts + tests/unit/url.test.ts; no replaceState/pushState anywhere in app/src; all navigation is hash-based; FilterModel's serializeFilterModel/deserializeFilterModel are used only for settings persistence (SettingsFilters.svelte, App.svelte:103), never for URLs. So urlHasFilters is always false for any URL the current app can produce, and the documented "deep link wins" contract is vacuous. Severity low is appropriate: no current user-facing breakage (personal defaults still apply correctly in the only reachable scenario), but the latent clobbering risk and false-confidence unit tests (filter-model.test.ts:252-255 test the pure function, not the wiring) are real.
