Appendix A — Refuted findings

Reported by a reviewer, then rejected by the adversarial verifiers. Kept for transparency.

mediumRefuted frontend effort: medium

Retired parcel-filter pipeline (filter-engine.ts, query.ts, most of filters.ts/url.ts, DuckDB parcel methods) is runtime-dead but still maintained and tested

app/src/lib/filter-engine.ts:1-123 · reviewer: frontend-quality
What it is
The old per-state parcel filter chain survives in full even though stores.svelte.ts states it was retired: (1) app/src/lib/filter-engine.ts (123 lines) is imported by nothing except tests/unit/filter-engine.test.ts — confirmed via the code graph (importers_of returns only the test). (2) app/src/lib/query.ts (composeWhere, countQuery, idQuery, ownerSearchQuery, parcelRowQuery) is imported only by duckdb.ts. (3) The DuckDbEngine parcel methods it feeds — loadState, count, matchingIds, searchOwners, fetchParcel (duckdb.ts lines 97-162) — have zero runtime callers (graph: callers_of loadState = 0; fetchParcel's only caller is the stale e2e suite via the window.__duckDb handle; the only live duckdb.ts consumer is towers.ts calling loadTowerPoints). (4) In app/src/lib/filters.ts, FilterState, emptyFilters, all five spec builders, FILTER_SPECS, isAnyFilterActive and activeFilterCount are runtime-reachable only through this dead chain plus url.ts — only sqlString/sqlNumber are live (imported by filter-model.ts). (5) In app/src/lib/url.ts, encodeFilters and buildUrl have no callers at all; the single live call is App.svelte:101 `readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null`, a boolean check on a legacy `?filters=` query param that nothing in the app writes anymore (no URLSearchParams writer outside Stats/tasks/activity exists). Five unit-test files (filter-engine, filter-spec, filters, query, url) keep ~529 lines of dead source green.
Why it matters
Roughly 600+ lines of source plus five test files are dead weight that every future contributor (and the schema-completeness/filter work) must read around. Worse, the live FilterModel in filter-model.ts was visibly copy-derived from filters.ts (same spec-builder shapes, same serialize-diff-against-defaults pattern), so two parallel filter vocabularies now coexist and invite edits to the wrong one. The App.svelte deep-link check also silently never fires: 'a shared deep link always wins' (App.svelte:97) can never happen because no code produces a `?filters=` URL — the saved-filter-default gate is keyed to a retired URL format.
How to fix
Delete filter-engine.ts, query.ts, and url.ts plus their unit tests; delete the parcel-query methods (loadState, count, matchingIds, searchOwners, fetchParcel) and the REGISTERED_FILE/queryChain machinery from duckdb.ts, keeping only init/loadTowerPoints; shrink filters.ts to sqlString/sqlNumber (or move those two into filter-model.ts and delete the file). Replace App.svelte's readUrlState check with a direct `new URLSearchParams(location.search).has('filters')` if the legacy-deep-link guard should survive, or drop the check and decide whether the new FilterModel should actually be URL-serialized (serializeFilterModel already exists).
Expected improvement
Removes ~700 lines of dead source plus five obsolete test files, leaves exactly one filter vocabulary (FILTER_FIELDS in filter-model.ts), and makes the saved-filter-default boot path honest about deep links.
Evidence
stores.svelte.ts:9-10: "The per-state catalog / slim-parquet / DuckDB parcel-fade machinery was retired with the state selector." — graph: importers_of('app/src/lib/filter-engine.ts') → only tests/unit/filter-engine.test.ts; callers_of(DuckDbEngine.loadState) → 0; App.svelte:101: `const urlHasFilters = readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null;` is url.ts's only runtime call site.
Verifier notes (2)
  • REFUTE (existence lens, high confidence): This finding duplicates PERF-030 of the 2026-06-07 perf audit ("Dead parcel-filter code path retained in the main bundle — orphaned FilterEngine/url + retained DuckDB parcel-query methods", docs/PERF_AUDIT_2026-06-07.md lines 592-606). PERF-030 names the same files (filter-engine.ts whole file, url.ts, query.ts:36-65 builders, duckdb.ts:97-162 loadState/count/matchingIds/searchOwners/fetchParcel with only loadTowerPoints live, the FilterState half of filters.ts) and the same fix (delete filter-engine.ts + url.ts, remove the parcel methods, prune query.ts/filters.ts). Per the verifier rails, duplication of one of the 42 audit findings means exists=false. Moreover, PERF-030 was not merely listed — it was explicitly considered and DEFERRED in commit c47e184: the commit message says "Deferred (with reasons in the audit hand-off): ... 030 (premise stale — modules are test-live) ...", i.e. the team consciously decided not to delete because the modules are kept green by the unit suite — exactly the situation this finding re-reports. For the record, the underlying facts ARE all true in the current code (I verified each): app/src/lib/filter-engine.ts is 123 lines and imported only by app/tests/unit/filter-engine.test.ts; query.ts (65 lines) is imported by duckdb.ts AND by the dead filter-engine.ts (the finding said "only duckdb.ts" — minor correction); duckDb's loadState/count/matchingIds/searchOwners/fetchParcel (duckdb.ts:97-162) have zero src callers — the only live consumer is towers.ts:143 calling loadTowerPoints, and tests/e2e/duckdb.spec.ts exercises count/matchingIds/fetchParcel via window.__duckDb (duckdb.ts:212-214); in filters.ts only sqlString/sqlNumber are live (filter-model.ts:29); url.ts's encodeFilters/buildUrl have no callers; App.svelte:101 `readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null` is the only runtime url.ts call and grep finds no writer of a `?filters=` param anywhere in src; the five test files (filter-engine, filter-spec, filters, query, url .test.ts) all exist in app/tests/unit/; the stores.svelte.ts:9-10 "retired" quote is verbatim. One sub-claim is genuinely novel relative to PERF-030: the App.svelte:96-104 saved-filter-default gate ("a shared deep link always wins") is keyed to the retired `?filters=` URL format that nothing writes, so urlHasFilters is always false except for pre-redesign bookmarks — PERF-030 actually (wrongly) stated url.ts is "never imported" and missed this call site. If the reviewer wants to keep anything, that fragment (the dead deep-link guard in bootUserSettings, plus the fact that the new FilterModel is not URL-serialized at all) could stand as a separate small finding; the bulk of this one is the already-triaged-and-deferred PERF-030 work order.
  • REFUTE (impact lens, high confidence): Verified the technical substance and it is accurate, but the finding duplicates PERF-030 of the 2026-06-07 perf audit, so per the verification rails it is exists=false. What I checked: (1) grep of all importers — app/src/lib/filter-engine.ts is imported only by app/tests/unit/filter-engine.test.ts; query.ts only by duckdb.ts and filter-engine.ts; url.ts only by App.svelte:22 (readUrlState); filters.ts only by filter-engine.ts/url.ts/query.ts plus filter-model.ts:29 importing just sqlString/sqlNumber. (2) duckdb.ts (read in full): the only src caller of any duckDb method is towers.ts:143 → loadTowerPoints; loadState/count/matchingIds/searchOwners/fetchParcel are reached only by the stale e2e suite (app/tests/e2e/duckdb.spec.ts via window.__duckDb — issue #92 acknowledges that suite is stale). (3) App.svelte:95-105 — readUrlState(location.search).filters !== null at line 101 is url.ts's sole runtime call; no pushState/replaceState/encodeFilters/buildUrl callers anywhere in src, and routing is hash-based, so nothing writes ?filters= anymore (the "deep link wins" branch can only fire on a legacy bookmarked URL, and is behaviorally harmless either way). (4) The five test files (filter-engine, filter-spec, filters, query, url .test.ts) exist in app/tests/unit. (5) stores.svelte.ts:9-10 retirement comment confirmed verbatim. Line counts: filter-engine 123, query 65, url 82, filters 192. Why exists=false: this is PERF-030 in docs/PERF_AUDIT_2026-06-07.md (lines 590-605) — same modules, same dead-method list (loadState/count/matchingIds/searchOwners/fetchParcel at duckdb.ts:97-162), and a near-identical execution plan ("delete filter-engine.ts + url.ts; remove the parcel methods; prune query.ts builders and the FilterState half of filters.ts"). The rails state that a duplicate of any of the audit's 42 findings is exists=false. Moreover, PERF-030 was explicitly considered and DEFERRED in commit c47e184 ("Deferred ... 030 (premise stale — modules are test-live)"), so this is a known, consciously-parked item, not a new discovery. Corrections/additions for the record: (a) the finding is actually more current than the audit on one point — the audit says url.ts is "imported by nothing," but App.svelte has imported readUrlState since PRD #121; the finding correctly accounts for that. (b) The only genuinely novel fragment — the App.svelte:101 saved-filter-default gate being keyed to a URL format nothing produces — is real but info-level on its own: since urlHasFilters is effectively always false, saved defaults simply always apply when configured, which is benign. (c) The c47e184 deferral rationale ("modules are test-live") is arguably circular — the tests are the only consumers — so if the team wants to re-open this, the finding's deletion plan is sound; but as a review finding it is a re-statement of a documented, deliberately-deferred audit item.

Appendix C — Method & coverage

22 scoped reviewers each owned a subsystem × dimension slice (correctness, security, duplication/simplification, error handling, tests, new-perf-only, deps/config, cross-subsystem contract drift, PII/data handling, deploy/infra), briefed on the project's intentional invariants (per-request users.txt authority, teams-as-tenants, no normalize-time filtering, EPSG:4326, atomic writes, Qt-only-in-puller) so designs aren't reported as bugs — only violations of them. A completeness critic then audited coverage and spawned 4 extra reviewers (credentials-file runtime lifecycle, composite-publish seam, identity/tenancy lifecycle, builder→browser artifact contract). Findings were semantically deduplicated, then adversarially verified (2 verifiers per finding + tiebreaker). Every finding cites file:line plus a verbatim evidence snippet read from the code. Total: 346 agents spawned, ~10.6M tokens, 106 minutes before the run was stopped.
